  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Suleiman, leader of The Ottomans (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Ottomans
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Janissary, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Siege Foundry, Replacing Forge (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Improvement: Tersane (Unique Ottoman Great Person Tile Improvement. Can be built by Expending a Great Admiral or embarked Great General. Must be built in water next to a City, but not next to another Tersane.
          Provides Gold, Production, Science, and Great Admiral Points. +25% Production and +15 Experience for Naval Units built in the Adjacent City. Does not stack with multiple Tersane next to the same City.
          Building a Tersane claims adjacent tiles. Units standing on a Tersane receive +50% Tile Defense. Deals 30 damage to adjacent Enemy Units.); Requires technology: None
        - Civilization Ability: Completing a Trade Route grants +150 Food and Science to the origin City if International, or Gold and Culture if Internal. Bonuses scale with Era.
      - Leader: Suleiman
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Fiefdoms (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +15% Production and +100% Border Growth during "We Love the King Day."
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 5
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 4
        - Boldness: 4
        - WarBias: 5
        - HostileBias: 8
        - WarmongerHate: 5
        - NeutralBias: 4
        - FriendlyBias: 4
        - GuardedBias: 7
        - AfraidBias: 5
        - DiplomaticBalance: 10
        - Friendliness: 4
        - WorkWithWillingness: 8
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 9
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 5
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 3
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 3
        - DenounceWillingness: 6
        - Forgiveness: 4
        - Meanness: 6
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 5
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (Japan destroyed 2 Destroyers this turn and has Tanks advancing on our northern front - direct escalation. Boldness reduced to 4 (from 5) as we cannot risk offensive operations when Japan's military (21,563) is 3x ours (7,057). WarBias increased to 5 (from 4) because we must be ready to defend aggressively against Japan's Tank push. HostileBias increased to 8 (from 7) - Japan has proven hostile aggression. GuardedBias 7 (from 6) reflects increased caution after losing naval units. AfraidBias increased to 5 (from 4) - Japan's Information Era tanks and fleet are a genuine existential threat. Meanness increased to 6 (from 5) - may need harsher tactics including nuclear response. WorkAgainstWillingness 9 maintains coalition focus against Japan's expansion.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Penicillin
        - Rationale: Japan captured Kayseri with Tanks/Modern Armor and destroyed our Dreadnought - existential threat intensifying. Need Marines for amphibious defense and Medical Labs for population growth/sustainability. Penicillin also leads to Electronics which helps with radar/communications. Nuclear Fission still 10 turns away - need all military advantages now.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: Increasing Egypt hostility to -25/-25. Egypt is Japan's vassal, actively funding Japan's war effort (31 Alumunium in Egypt territory fuels Japan's air/industrial capacity). Diplomatic flexibility unnecessary when enemy status enforced by vassalage. Higher hostility directs AI to consider Egypt as legitimate target while maintaining Jordan borders securing flank during Greece campaign.
        - UpdatedTurn: 356
        
        ### Greece
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Greece is now our vassal (capitulated turn 375), yet relationships remain at -25/-25 from when they were enemies. This contradictory setting prevents proper vassal-master coordination. Increasing to +40/+40 reflects the correct vassal relationship, acknowledges their contribution of 52 GPT and resources, and stops AI from inappropriately treating our vassal as hostile target.
        - UpdatedTurn: 377
        
        ### Morocco
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Morocco maintained at +40/+40 as our vassal. Critical ally providing 17 Aluminum, 52 GPT, and valuable trade routes. Trade route to Marrakech completed successfully (event 4), generating civilization bonuses. Morocco actively engaged against Indonesia combat near Egyptian lands, demonstrating vassal loyalty. Morocco's declaration of war on Indonesia (event 127 by Byblos, Morocco ally) aligns with our interests. Consolidating vassal relations important for coalition against Japan and maintaining Arabia front stability.
        - UpdatedTurn: 381
        
        ### Denmark
        - Public: -75
        - Private: -75
        - Rationale: Denmark declared war on us after Japan liberated them (turn 398 event 180). Opportunistic betrayal while we face existential Japan threat. With Oslo and Tunsberg lost (our former cities, now Danish), Denmark represents northern vulnerability. Relations set to -75/-75 for aggressive stance - need to eliminate Denmark before they join Japanese offensives or disrupt northern front. Cannot tolerate hostile Denmark behind our lines while fighting Japan.
        - UpdatedTurn: 399
        
        ### Indonesia
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -40
        - Rationale: Indonesia at -40/-40 (from -30/-30). Japan's war declaration changes strategic calculus - cannot afford two-front major war. Indonesia is secondary threat - conclusion of Indonesia war must accelerate to focus resources on Japan defense. Reducing from -40 maintains hostile stance but slightly de-escalates to allow concentration on Japan. Once Japan neutralized, Indonesia becomes primary target for conquest.
        - UpdatedTurn: 399
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -90
        - Private: -90
        - Rationale: Japan maximum hostility increased to -90/-90 (from -80/-80) after capturing Sale Turn 405. Japan destroyed our Dreadnought ID 17195 and Destroyer ID 15276 in same turn. Japan's naval superiority (Fleet Destroyers, Yamato) and air advantage (Jets, B17s) demonstrated in simultaneous land (Sale capture) and naval victories. With 68 techs vs our 62, 19,784 vs 7,167 military, and foothold in Sale, Japan is immediate existential threat. Increased hostility ensures AI treats Japan as absolute priority for military targeting and resource allocation, maintains sanctions effectiveness, and prevents any diplomatic accommodation while we develop nuclear deterrence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 406
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Japan captured Kayseri - must urgently strengthen northern defenses. Defense 95, CityDefense 95, Antiair 95 against Japan's air superiority. Mobilization 100 and Production 100 for rapid military buildup. Nuke/UseNuke maxed - nuclear deterrence critical with Nuclear Fission in 10 turns. Science 100 maintains tech race. Gold 90 supports unit upgrades. Offense reduced to 20 - cannot afford aggression when facing superior Japanese Tank/Modern Armor push.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 43

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Sanction: Japan:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Greek Sphere of Influence: Genoa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Brussels:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1

            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Japanese Sphere of Influence: Rio de Janeiro:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Spaceflight Regulations:
            - Description: Spaceflight Regulations control the proliferation of modern ballistic technology, oversee national space programs, and limit the advance of pontentially-dangerous technology.
              As long as this resolution is active, all players will suffer a global -25% Production penalty for Spaceship Parts and +25% Gold purchase costs for Spaceship Parts.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: International Space Station:
            - Description: Begins the International Space Station project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : International Space Station wonder appears in Capital. (+1 Production from Scientists, and +1 Science from Engineers. Great Scientists provide 33% more Science when used to discover new Technology. +100 in City where it is built when you unlock a new Technology, scaling with Era.)
              : Free Great Scientist.
              : One time Research boost.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Rocketry.
            - Proposer: Japan
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Indonesia
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Greece

        ### 229

        You, Suleiman (leader of The Ottomans, Player 4), are making strategic decisions after turn 409.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Suleiman, leader of The Ottomans (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Ottomans
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Janissary, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Siege Foundry, Replacing Forge (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Improvement: Tersane (Unique Ottoman Great Person Tile Improvement. Can be built by Expending a Great Admiral or embarked Great General. Must be built in water next to a City, but not next to another Tersane.
          Provides Gold, Production, Science, and Great Admiral Points. +25% Production and +15 Experience for Naval Units built in the Adjacent City. Does not stack with multiple Tersane next to the same City.
          Building a Tersane claims adjacent tiles. Units standing on a Tersane receive +50% Tile Defense. Deals 30 damage to adjacent Enemy Units.); Requires technology: None
        - Civilization Ability: Completing a Trade Route grants +150 Food and Science to the origin City if International, or Gold and Culture if Internal. Bonuses scale with Era.
      - Leader: Suleiman
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Fiefdoms (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +15% Production and +100% Border Growth during "We Love the King Day."
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: keep-status-quo
      - rationale: Japan continues existential offensives with overwhelming military superiority (22,557 vs 7,255). Captured Sale, destroyed Dreadnought and Destroyer. Nuclear Fission in 9 turns remains critical path to survival. Current defensive emergency flavors (Defense 95, CityDefense 95, Mobilization 100, Production 100, Antiair 95, Nuke 100, UseNuke 98) appropriately prioritize survival and nuclear deterrent. No change to grand strategy or flavors warranted while existential threat persists.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Radar
        - Rationale: Penicillin research completed (provides Medical Labs and Marines). Nuclear Fission is 9 turns away and remains priority. After Nuclear Fission, Radar is essential because Japan has overwhelming air superiority (Jets, B17s, Bombers) devastating our land and naval forces. Fighters will provide critical city air defense and air interception capability. Heavy Bombers also unlock for future offensive operations once nuclear deterrent is established.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Patriotic War
        - Rationale: Order Level 1 tenet Patriotic War provides immediate +20% combat strength for land units near or in cities, which is critical against Japan's Tank and Modern Armor offensives. The heal-on-conquest bonus sustains offensive capabilities when retaking cities, and T-34 unlock provides strong defensive armored units. This is superior to Spaceflight Pioneers (survival takes precedence over long-term science) and Communism (military bonuses more urgent than economic).

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Current State:**
            - Turn 409
            - The Ottomans (Player 4), Suleiman, Atomic Era
            - Current Research: Nuclear Fission (9 turns remaining, so should be completing around turn 418)
            - Next Policy: 16 turns away
            - We have 17 cities, Population 398
            - Military Strength: 7,255 (down from 7,167 last turn? Actually wait, that says 7255 now)
            - Gold: 1,218, GPT: 386
            - Science: 2,203, Culture: 2,656, Faith: 553
            - 83/84 military units (supply nearly maxed)
            - We are at war with Japan (score -44%, war weariness 28%), Indonesia (score 14%, weariness 34%), Denmark (score 54%, weariness 3%)
            - We have vassals: Greece (was at +40/+40, but actually Greece was defeated? Wait, looking at the players section: Japan defeated Greece. But we still have Morocco as vassal)
            - Wait, looking at the Opponents section: Our relationships say Morocco is "Our Vassal (Capitulated)" with Open Borders and Declaration of Friendship
            - But there's no Greece listed as a player anymore. Actually looking at the Victory Progress, Indonesia controls Athens and Jakarta. Japan controls Kyoto, Copenhagen, Thebes, Mecca. Ottomans control Istanbul and Marrakech. So Greece has been eliminated as a major civ.
            - We have the United Nations built in Istanbul!
            
            **Major Threats:**
            1. **Japan**: Military 22,557 (up from 21,563), 69 techs (up from 68), Era: Information, 39 cities, population 811. They captured Sale (from us or puppet?), and are pushing aggressively. They have Fleet Destroyers, Yamato battleships, Tanks, B17s, Bombers, Triplanes. They declared war on us and have captured some of our cities in the past (Kayseri, Sale mentioned). War score 44% in their favor, our war weariness 28%.
               - They have sphere of influence over multiple city-states: Vienna, Lhasa, Rio de Janeiro, M'banza-Kongo, Genoa, Enns, Abernethy.
               - They have 50 delegates (need 43 for diplo victory) - they are very close to diplo victory! World Congress voting in 3 turns?
               - Wait, "World Congress - Voting in 3 turns" means we're about to have a World Leader election potentially? With 50 delegates and needing 43, Japan could win diplomatic victory in 3 turns!
            
            2. **Indonesia**: Military 5,191, 67 techs, 10 cities. At war with us. War score 14% in our favor. We're actually doing okay against them militarily.
            
            3. **Denmark**: Military 195 (pathetic), 2 cities, our war score 54%. They are Japan's vassal. Can be ignored mostly but we should finish them off if possible.
            
            **Critical Issue: Diplomatic Victory**
            Japan has 50 delegates, needs 43. The World Congress votes in 3 turns. If nothing changes, Japan will win Diplomatic Victory unless we can prevent it.
            
            But wait - "Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission." World Ideology: Freedom was enacted? No, "World Ideology: Freedom" is listed as EnactedOn: -1. The United Nations has been constructed (by us, in Istanbul). Diplomatic victory should be enabled.
            
            Actually, to win Diplomatic Victory, you need to be elected World Leader. Japan has 50/43 needed. But the vote happens in the World Congress. Can we stop Japan from winning?
            
            Looking at active resolutions:
            - Sanction: Japan (This helps - provides 0.5x warmonger penalty for attacking them, blocks trade)
            - Sanction: The Ottomans (This hurts us)
            - Global Peace Accords (Unit maintenance +25%, 2x warmonger penalties)
            
            Wait, the "VotesNeeded: 43" - is that the number needed to win? Yes. Japan has 50 delegates. They have more than needed.
            
            But the World Congress proposals for this session:
            - Japan proposed: International Space Station
            - Indonesia proposed: International Games
            - Greece proposed: Decolonization
            
            There's no "World Leader" vote listed in the proposals. Hmm. In Civ V, World Leader votes happen every 20 turns after the first proposal. Maybe the "Voting in 3 turns" is for these proposals, not necessarily World Leader? Or maybe these are the proposals and the World Leader vote is automatic.
            
            Actually, looking at the options for policies, "Spaceflight Pioneers" is a Level 3 Tenet of Order. We are Order ideology.
            
            Wait, we adopted Order! But in the player profile under "Relationships", Japan says "You have chosen to adopt the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Order" - that's THEIR view of us? No wait, looking at Japan's relationship: "You have chosen to adopt the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." That makes sense. Japan has Freedom.
            
            And in our Ottoman profile, under "TheirOpinionToUs" for Japan: "You have chosen to adopt the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom."
            
            Looking at our policies: We have Order, Rationalism, Artistry, Statecraft, Imperialism, Progress, Authority, Fealty branches.
            
            So we are following Order.
            
            Denmark says "You have adopted the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Order" - that's Denmark's view. Wait Denmark is Order? And they say WE adopted Freedom? That contradicts our policy list. Actually looking at Denmark's info: "You have adopted the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Order." And we're at war. This is confusing. Maybe there's a bug or I'm misreading.
            
            Actually looking back at our policies, maybe we DID adopt Freedom? No, it clearly lists Order policies: Double Agents, Academy of Sciences, Dictatorship of the Proletariat, Workers' Faculties, Peace, Land, Bread.
            
            Wait the relationship modifiers:
            - Denmark's "TheirOpinionToUs": "You have chosen to adopt the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." So Denmark is Freedom.
            
            Japan's "TheirOpinionToUs": "You have chosen to adopt the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." So Japan is Freedom.
            
            Indonesia: "You have both chosen to adopt the Order Ideology. (999)" So Indonesia is Order.
            
            So we are Order. Indonesia is Order. Japan and Denmark are Freedom.
            
            The World Ideology is Freedom (enacted resolution). That gives Japan extra delegates. That's why Japan has so many.
            
            **Immediate Strategic Priorities:**
            
            1. **Survival against Japan**: Their military is 3x ours. They have better tech (Information vs Atomic). They just destroyed our Dreadnought and Destroyer, captured Sale. They have tanks, modern armor, jets, B17s, Yamato battleships.
            
            2. **Diplomatic Victory prevention**: Japan has 50 delegates, needs 43. The World Congress meets in 3 turns. If Japan wins World Leader, game over. Can we prevent this?
               - We have 15 delegates. Morocco has 7. Our vassal states might vote for us? Actually in Civ V, vassals vote for their master in World Leader elections. Morocco is our vassal, so they should give us their 7 votes? No wait, vassals give their delegates to their master? Actually I think vassal city-states give their votes, but player vassals might not. Actually in BNW/Diplo Victory, only city-states give delegates. The numbers shown might include our base delegates plus city-state allies.
               - Looking at city-states:
                 - Lutetia: Our ally (542 influence, protected)
                 - Brussels: Friends (58 influence)
                 - Genoa: War (-60)
                 - Others are allied with Japan or Indonesia.
               - We need to somehow get more delegates or prevent Japan from keeping theirs. But with only 3 turns, we probably can't sway enough city-states. However, if Japan doesn't get World Leader this time (maybe because there's no World Leader proposal this session?), we have more time.
            
            Wait, looking at the proposals again: There's no "Elect World Leader" proposal. The proposals are:
            - Enact: International Space Station (Japan)
            - Enact: International Games (Indonesia)
            - Enact: Decolonization (Greece)
            
            These are standard resolutions. The World Leader vote might happen on a different schedule or already happened. But the status says "World Congress - Voting in 3 turns."
            
            Wait, "Diplomatic Victory" says:
            - VotesNeeded: 43
            - Status: World Congress - Voting in 3 turns
            
            This strongly implies there IS a World Leader vote in 3 turns. But it's not listed in Proposals? Maybe the UI doesn't list it as a "proposal" because it's automatic.
            
            If Japan has 50/43 in 3 turns, we are about to **lose the game** to diplomatic victory unless:
            - We can somehow reduce Japan's delegates below 43 in 3 turns. Impossible.
            - We can win the vote ourselves. We have 15, Morocco has 7 (but might vote for themselves or us?). Even combined that's 22. Not enough.
            - The vote requires a majorityof all delegates or just the target? In Civ V you need a certain number of delegates to win World Leader. If multiple candidates, the one with the most and above the threshold wins? Actually in Civ V BNW, you need to reach the delegate threshold. If no one does, no one wins.
            
            But we can't prevent Japan from winning diplomatically in 3 turns through normal means.
            
            Wait! **We have Nuclear Fission in 9 turns.** Once ANY civilization discovers Nuclear Fission, the World Ideology resolution becomes available. But more importantly, if we get nukes, we might be able to... well, nuking doesn't stop diplomatic victories directly.
            
            Actually, can we capture Japan's capital? Unlikely given their military strength.
            
            Can we capture city-states allied with Japan to reduce their delegates? Let's see Japan's city-state allies:
            - M'banza-Kongo: Japan Ally
            - Rio de Janeiro: Japan Ally
            - Lhasa: Japan Ally
            - Abernethy: Japan Ally
            - Enns: Japan Ally
            - Genoa: Japan Ally
            - Vienna: Japan Ally
            
            Some of these are far away (Rio, Lhasa, Enns, Vienna, Abernethy). We are at war with some (Genoa). But we can't reach most of them in 3 turns.
            
            Hmm. Actually, wait. Is "VotesNeeded: 43" for World Leader? Or is it for resolutions? Looking at the Victory Progress section, it's under "DiplomaticVictory". Japan has 50 delegates and needs 43. They have 116% of needed.
            
            In Civ V, if a player has more delegates than needed, they win the World Leader vote automatically when it comes up.
            
            We have 3 turns. What can we do?
            
            Actually, I notice that the World Ideology is Freedom. If we switch our ideology... no, we can't switch quickly.
            
            Wait - could we **liberate** a city-state that Japan has conquered to reduce their delegates? Or **conquer a city-state allied with Japan**? If Japan loses city-state allies, they lose delegates. But as noted, most are far.
            
            Actually, looking at the events, Kyzyl used to be our ally but now it's allied with... wait, event 132 says "SetAlly OldAllyPlayer: 4, MinorPlayer: 27". Kyzyl is Player 27. So Kyzyl is no longer our ally. Then event 0 on Turn 409: "DeclareWar IsAggressor: true TargetTeam: Player_4 OriginatingPlayer: 27". So Kyzyl declared war on us! That means we lost that alliance.
            
            But even if we somehow got Kyzyl back, that's just a few delegates.
            
            Wait, looking at Denmark: they are Japan's vassal. If we conquer Denmark's cities... but they only have 2 cities. 
            
            Actually, I think we need to prepare for the possibility that Japan wins Diplomatic Victory in 3 turns. But the system says "Voting in 3 turns" - perhaps the vote is for the proposals listed, not World Leader. Or perhaps the game continues if no one reaches the threshold? But Japan is already above the threshold.
            
            Actually, in Civ V, World Leader elections happen every 20 turns after the first one. Maybe this session is not a World Leader session? The status says "World Congress - Voting in 3 turns" but doesn't explicitly say World Leader.
            
            Wait, there's an active resolution "World Ideology: Freedom" which is "Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission." It says EnactedOn: -1, which means it's currently active (enacted). The United Nations has been constructed. Once UN is built and World Ideology is enacted, World Leader votes happen.
            
            I think the game might end in 3 turns if we don't do something drastic. But what drastic thing can we do? We could try to capture enough of Japan's cities or city-states to reduce their delegate count, but that's impossible in 3 turns.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully: the "Contender" for Diplomatic Victory is Japan. They have 50 delegates. But perhaps the "VotesNeeded" assumes all civilizations are present. If some civilizations are eliminated, the threshold might change? No, in Civ V it's fixed based on number of civs and city-states.
            
            Hmm. Actually, could we liberate a civilization to increase the total delegates needed? If we liberate a civ, the threshold goes up and maybe Japan drops below it? But Japan has 50. The threshold is 43. Even if it went up by a few, Japan might still be over. And we don't have anyone to liberate easily.
            
            Wait! What if we **denounce** or **go to war** with city-states... no, that doesn't reduce Japan's delegates unless we conquer them.
            
            I think we might be facing game over in 3 turns unless there's some mechanic I'm missing. But let me look at the available tools. We can:
            - set-persona
            - set-relationship
            - set-flavors
            - set-research
            - set-policy
            - keep-status-quo
            
            None of these directly prevent a diplomatic victory. But we need to play as if we have a chance. Perhaps the World Leader vote won't happen this session, or perhaps the AI/staff members can do something tactical.
            
            Actually, looking at the events again, something interesting happened:
            - Turn 408, event 131-132: Kyzyl (Player 27) changed ally from us to... wait, it says "OldAllyPlayer: 4, MinorPlayer: 27" then "SetAlly". But it doesn't specify new ally. Then on Turn 409, Kyzyl declared war on us. So maybe Kyzyl became allied with Japan or Indonesia.
            
            If Japan wins in 3 turns, none of our long-term planning matters. But I should plan for survival.
            
            Current research is Nuclear Fission (9 turns). We just completed Penicillin. Units we created this turn: Submarine, Destroyer (x3), Triplane, Anti-Aircraft Gun (x2), Infantry (upgraded from Rifleman).
            
            Our flavors currently emphasize Defense, Mobilization, CityDefense, Antiair, Nuke, etc.
            
            Should we change research? Nuclear Fission is 9 turns away and will unlock the Manhattan Project and Atomic Bombs. Given that Japan is about to potentially win diplomatically or overrun us militarily, we need:
            1. Radar (for Fighters, Heavy Bombers)
            2. Computers (for Carriers, extra trade route)
            3. Electronics (for Police Stations)
            
            But Nukes are our only potential equalizer against Japan's overwhelming military. With 19 Uranium, Japan could nuke us. We have only 3 Uranium. But if we get Nuclear Fission, we can build nukes.
            
            Actually, we completed Penicillin, which was our queued research. So now we need to set the next research. Nuclear Fission is currently being researched (9 turns left). So set-research is for AFTER Nuclear Fission.
            
            Wait, the Event says: "TeamTechResearched Tech: Penicillin" on event 84 of Turn 408. And our strategy says "Next: Penicillin" but we just researched it. Actually, looking at the current state: "CurrentResearch: Nuclear Fission (Estimated in 9 turns)". So Penicillin was completed and now we're on Nuclear Fission. The set-research tool is for AFTER completing the current one.
            
            What should come after Nuclear Fission?
            - Radar -> Advanced Ballistics -> Mobile Tactics
            - Computers -> Telecommunications -> Satellites; also unlocks Carrier
            - Electronics -> Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
            
            Given Japan's air superiority (B17s, Jets, Bombers), we need Fighters and advanced air defense. Radar unlocks Fighter and Heavy Bomber. Computers unlocks Carrier. 
            
            But the most urgent thing is: we have 9 turns until Nuclear Fission. The World Congress votes in 3 turns. We might lose before then. But assuming we survive, what next?
            
            Actually, wait. I realize I may have misread the diplomatic situation. "World Congress - Voting in 3 turns" might refer to the proposals listed, and the World Leader vote might be separate. Let me re-read:
            
            "DiplomaticVictory
            - VotesNeeded: 43
            - Status: World Congress - Voting in 3 turns
            - ActiveResolutions: ..."
            
            Hmm. Actually in Vox Populi (which this is), the diplomatic victory mechanics might be different. It might require building the United Nations and then getting enough votes for a resolution. But we already built it.
            
            Actually, in the base game with Vox Populi, diplomatic victory might work differently. But given the interface says "VotesNeeded: 43" and Japan has 50, they're dangerously close.
            
            Let me focus on what we CAN control.
            
            **Military Situation:**
            Our land zones near Indonesia (Denpasar, etc.) show we're actually doing okay against Indonesia. We killed some of their units. Morocco is fighting Indonesia too.
            
            Against Japan: Our sea zones near Casablanca and Huari show enemy naval presence. Japan destroyed our Dreadnought at Casablanca and an Artillery at Huari. We have subs and destroyers.
            
            Our northern front: We need to defend against Japan's tanks and mechanized forces. Our cities listed: Casablanca took damage, but we have strong forces there (Landship, Machine Guns, Artillery).
            
            **Recommended Actions:**
            
            1. **Persona**: Keep fairly defensive but maybe slightly more willingness to use nukes. Boldness is 4. We should maybe keep it there. We can't afford to be too aggressive. WarBias 5 is fine.
            
            2. **Research**: After Nuclear Fission, we need Radar for fighters to counter Japan's air force. Or Computers for carriers and extra trade route. Given Japan's B17s and our lack of fighters, Radar seems critical. But actually, we have Triplanes. Radar gives us Fighters (air superiority) and Heavy Bombers. Let's set Radar.
            
            3. **Policy**: We have 16 turns until next policy. We have many branches open. We could go for a Level 3 Order tenet like Spaceflight Pioneers (for Great Engineer/Scientist and spaceship parts) or Socialist Realism or Iron Curtain. Given we're at war and not going for culture, Spaceflight Pioneers seems best if we pivot to science victory. But we just built UN in Istanbul... wait, we built the UN! That means we should have triggered the World Leader vote.

          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Current State:**
            - Turn 409 just completed
            - I'm playing as The Ottomans (Suleiman), Player 4
            - Current Era: Atomic
            - Current Research: Nuclear Fission (9 turns remaining)
            - Grand Strategy: Conquest
            - Military Strength: 7,255 vs Japan's 22,557
            
            **Key Threats:**
            1. **Japan**: Information Era, 69 techs, military strength 22,557 (3x ours), controls 50% of capitals. They captured Sale, destroyed our Dreadnought and Destroyer. They have Tanks, Jets, B17s, Yamato battleships. Maximum hostility (-90/-90).
            2. **Indonesia**: Information Era, 67 techs, military 5,191. At war with us (score: 14% our favor, but they have higher war weariness 34% vs our 28% with Japan). Relationship -40/-40. They are secondary threat.
            3. **Denmark**: Japan's vassal, weak (military 195), but at war with us. Captured Oslo and Tunsberg (our former cities). -75/-75.
            
            **Recent Events:**
            - We researched Penicillin (unlocks Marines, Medical Labs)
            - We built several units: Anti-Aircraft Guns, Submarines, Destroyers, Triplanes
            - We lost a Dreadnought and Destroyer to Japan
            - We killed a Japanese Fleet Destroyer
            - We killed an Indonesian Infantry
            - We built Military Academy in Denpasar, Broadcast Tower in Kuala Lumpur
            - We placed a Manufactory with Great Engineer
            - We're quarrying Uranium tiles (strategic for nukes!)
            
            **Diplomatic Position:**
            - Both Japan and The Ottomans are sanctioned by the World Congress
            - World Ideology is Freedom (we follow Order, Japan follows Freedom)
            - We're losing the diplomatic game badly - Japan has 50 delegates, we have 15
            - World Congress voting in 3 turns
            - Japan proposed International Space Station, Indonesia proposed International Games, Greece proposed Decolonization
            
            **Victory Prospects:**
